"Can you come back in five?" Agatha asks. The kid with the camera nods and takes off again, and Alice approaches slowly, uncharacteristically quiet as she eyes the finished piece.

Agatha runs a soaked towel over it, and Stevie hisses in what sounds like pleasure at the coldness on her overheated, swollen skin. Glancing up at Alice to see her face properly gives Agatha no hint as to how she's feeling, and her lack of usual compliments and detailed comments feels ice -cold in a way that leaves her squirming in her seat.

Finally, she gives a small nod with pursed lips and then turns to return to her own station. It's not a real comment, but it looks somewhat like approval. Before Agatha can ask her for feedback, feeling needy and on edge, Alice's own client arrives and her demeanour shifts to bright and cheery as she leads them over to her station.

Stevie makes playful grabby hands at the mirror that Agatha offers her, and she's viscerally reminded of something Billy had said to her once about 'the ick'—something she'd originally filed away as gen-z idiocy but was now beginning to grasp the merit of.

"Oh, I love him," she breathes, so starry-eyed and outwardly appreciative that Agatha forgets her annoyance immediately. "Holy shit, I need to come see you every year, seriously."

"You know where I'll be," Agatha replies gruffly, more just to have something to say than really meaning it. Yesterday she'd have been thrilled to have a regular seasonal client like Stevie on the roster, but today she feels so discombobulated from managing everyone else's feelings that now, when she's not being distracted by the whir of her tattoo machine and the focus of the work, all she really wants to do is take Nicky, take her cash, and go home to her own couch and television and a trashy old sci-fi movie.

"The Miss Salem Ink panel is starting soon!" Billy announces almost breathlessly as he bounds up to the booth. Rio is in tow but pointedly admiring the surrounding booths instead of meeting Agatha's eye. "Jen said she'll buy a round if Lilia makes anyone cry."

Alright, forget the couch and sci-fi flick. A cold beer would do just as well.

"I cannot believe how many numbers you got," Alice breathes, following Lilia and Jen as they step out of the biting evening air and into the front waiting area of the nearby sports grill that they'd chosen for its proximity to the convention hall.

She shuffles through the crumpled papers and business cards in awe before handing the stack back to Lilia, who surprises Agatha by tucking the whole lot back into her oversized pocket.

"You gonna add those to your Rolodex?" Agatha asks. Lilia lets her cane swing out a little further on her next stride, and Agatha barely latches herself onto Billy's shoulder in time as she catches her ankle on it.

He straightens her out with a warm smile of reassurance, but she simply averts her gaze and glances over at the sound of Nicky letting out another screeching giggle.

For the last half-block, he's been trying to hang his entire body weight off of Rio's arm to lift his feet from the ground, but each time he nearly has enough leverage, she tickles him into letting go, and the entire process starts over.

Agatha had been glad that whatever jealous tizzy Rio had been in earlier was quashed by Jen's assumption that she would be joining them for drinks. Her iciness toward Agatha hadn't warmed in the slightest in any meaningful way, but those quick few words—'Is Rio coming with us now, or meeting us?'—had been so natural and surprisingly un-teasing that Agatha hadn't had time to really register it, and neither had Rio.

Billy helpfully holds the door open for everyone once they make it to the restaurant, and Rio puts her hands almost sternly on Nicky's shoulders to lead him inside. Agatha can hardly believe her eyes when he settles immediately, following the wordless instruction to behave as the group of them make their way to the hostess stand.

"The numbers are less surprising to me than the politeness," Jen says, her voice carrying back over the group of them as they're led to a large round booth. "But at least I'm out of the woods on buying drinks."

"I think you rigged it," Alice accuses playfully. "Told Lilia early not to hurt any feelings."

"I would never deprive myself or my coven of such entertainment," Jen replies, faux-scandalized. She adjusts the skirt of her dress huffily as she slides into the booth.

It's a tight fit with all seven of them in a booth clearly made for five or six, but with Nicky hiked halfway onto Agatha's lap and Rio crammed against his other side, they make it work.

"I don't know why you all expect so little of me," Lilia says, hooking the end of her cane against the table ledge and tugging the cocktail menu closer to herself. Billy helpfully turns his phone flashlight on, aiming it for her even as he turns his attention back to Jen.

"It was a lot more diplomatic than I was expecting."

"You're just relieved you survived having to stare at that many tits for so long," Jen accuses.

Billy squacks in protest as the others laugh; Nicky sits up straighter as he realizes he missed a joke.

"I don't know if I'd call telling a girl that it was very brave to let an artist tattoo her mid-seizure 'diplomatic'," Agatha says.

"I didn't say it was her having the seizure, did I?" Lilia replies, "I could have meant the artist. That line work was abhorrent; I have no idea how they even qualified."

"Uh, probably because they have like, forty-seven thousand followers on TikTok," Alice says.

Lilia groans.

"Is that how it has to work now? We just let inadequacy slide—permanently alter people's bodies—just because someone managed to pick the right angles and catchy pop song to get popular at just the right time?"

"Here she goes," Jen mutters.

"You know, this is what's wrong with your generation—"

"Which one?" Rio asks unhelpfully.

Lilia replies without missing a beat in her rant.

"All of them. You're all missing the importance of tradition. Breaking rules before mastering them. That's why you're so lucky." She turns her attention to Billy. "Alice knows what she's doing. Between her and Lorna, rest her soul, you get to learn from two generations' worth of knowledge at once."

Agatha's mood instantly sours at the reminder of Billy's apprenticeship, and she flags their waitress down impatiently.

"I can't wait," Billy says, though his eager smile has softened modestly around the edges a little in a way that Agatha knows is her fault but can't bring herself to feel properly guilty about.

"Thank god," Agatha breathes as their server approaches. "I need something cold and out of a tap and upwards of the seven percent range. And nothing… pumpkin or whatever."

The waitress wordlessly turns to Rio as she makes a note on her order pad.

"Uh, whatever she's having," she says, turning to give Agatha a funny, wry little look of amusement as the server goes around the table to collect the remaining orders. 'You okay?' She mouths.

Agatha drops her gaze to Nicky instead and lifts his hand away from where he's picking at the marred varnish on the table, squeezing his small hand soothingly before putting it back in his lap.

Alice changes the trajectory of Lilia's rant and brings up the story of Lilia telling the winning tattoo model to 'relax, it's not the Nobel Prize' in response to their enthusiasm. By the time the drinks and a heaping plate of French fries arrive, the tension has melted just a little, and after their first round, it seems to have vanished completely. Agatha finds herself able to make eye contact with Alice again without feeling entirely iced out, and Jen even brings up something her client, Stevie, had said to another vendor that morning, which Jen had overheard.

"Oh, check this out," Billy interrupts them, turning his phone to Agatha. She wrestles it from his outstretched hand impatiently to squint at the screen and immediately recognizes a photo of the spidercrab tattoo.

"Best in show nominee?" She reads, causing Jen to snatch the phone out of her hands in surprise.

"Wow," the other woman breathes, looking genuinely impressed as she pinches at the screen to zoom in. "Look at that. That's good for the shop."

It's the closest thing Agatha's going to get to a compliment from her after the weekend they've had, so she takes it.

"Who else is in the running?" Alice asks, peeking over Jen's shoulder and then reaching around her cozily to scroll further. "Ugh, those dicks from Dark Craft, obviously."

Lilia mimes spitting and Billy winces, disgusted. Any more possible semi-compliments are halted as their waitress reappears, eyeing their nearly empty glasses.

When Agatha forgoes a second round of drinks, Rio follows suit, putting the pair of them in the crossfire of several pointed looks around the table. Agatha would normally call them on it, but Nicky's growing crankiness puts a damper on her plans to stay and goad them into finally saying the silent part out loud.

"Alright," she finally concedes, after even Rio's attempt to include Nicky doesn't seem to cheer him up. "I need to get him to bed."

Nicky whines a bit at that but doesn't protest when Rio slides out of the booth first and helps get him to his feet so Agatha can follow.

"I think that's my night, too," Jen says, slipping out on the opposite side and turning to Alice curiously.

"Oh, uh…" Alice glances between Lilia and Billy and then smiles tightly. "I think the three of us are going to stick around and talk some stuff out."

When Billy avoids Agatha's gaze too pointedly, she scoffs and shakes her head.

"Get home safe, then," she mutters, fussing for a moment over Nicky's coat sleeves and then following Jen and Rio toward the doors.

Jen pulls her phone from her pocket as they step outside and awkwardly floats further away from the three of them as she taps away on the screen for a few quiet moments. Agatha pulls her keys from her pocket as she watches Rio try to decide if she's going to ask if they're giving Jen a ride as well, but she doesn't get the chance when a shout across the street draws all of their attention.

Rio spots the cause first, swearing under her breath just as Agatha realizes what's going on. A man—the same man that Agatha had personally delivered photos of to every business within a three-block radius of Coven Ink—comes barrelling down the sidewalk on the opposite side of the street.

"Nicky, let's go pull the car around," Rio says suddenly, holding a hand out. He looks up at Agatha questioningly, but Rio has already ripped the car keys from her hand before she has the chance to reassure him herself.

Agatha's attention flies to Jen, anticipating her reaction, but she looks frozen in place, and the yelling isn't stopping.

"You ungrateful bitch, you wouldn't even have a job if it weren't for me, and now you're lying to whoever will listen to get me kicked out of work?"

Agatha sees Nicky look back over his shoulder in concern, but Rio continues to tug him away.

"I— I didn't—" Jen stumbles over her words, and for a rare moment, Agatha is at a loss for words as well. It's not often she sees Jen so genuinely scared, and it's discombobulating to face it twice in two days.

The man takes a step forward, one foot coming down off the curb as if he's going to cross over to them, and it spurs Agatha into action.

"Hey, back up," she demands, one hand sliding into her pocket. She knows full well that the only thing she's concealing is lint, her bar receipt, and a couple spare quarters for street parking, but the gesture has the intended effect of making the man hesitate.

He puts his hands up, but doesn't look any less sorry.

"I'm not doing anything; we're just talking. Or are you going to lie about that, too?"

Agatha's car revs unhealthily as it rounds the corner. She winces internally and makes a mental note not to let Rio get behind the wheel of it again in the future.

"She's not lying about anything; why don't you just fuck off before we all regret it?" Agatha threatens, her eyes flicking briefly to the car as it approaches.

Rio brings the nose up between the three of them, separating them in the narrow space of the side street, then lays on the horn so suddenly that it makes all three of them jump. Before Agatha can snap at her, the car inches up further still.

There's a soft crunch-pop that sounds like loose gravel being displaced under the car's tire, but a split second later, a loud yelp-turned-yell of pain tells them that Rio has rolled the tire over much more than just some rocks and cement.

"Fuck— FUCK! You're on my fucking foot, you crazy f-"

"Well, you're standing in the middle of the road, dumbass!" Rio yells back, cutting him off and flinging her hands up from the steering wheel in annoyance as if she's the one being inconvenienced.

"Back up! Back up!" He exclaims, banging on the hood.

"Hey! Don't hit my fucking car, you dick!" Rio yells back, grabbing the wheel again and inching the car further forward, entirely over the rest of his foot with another sickening series of crunches before he's finally unpinned.

He stumbles back and nearly topples into one of the wall planters before steadying himself and limping around the front of the car. His arms flail a little, and Agatha and Jen take a coordinated step out of range.

"Get in the car, get in the car," Agatha insists, pushing Jen toward the back door. Jen wrenches it open, and the two of them spill in beside a frightened, confused-looking Nicky.

"Hey, I got your fucking plates, you psycho!" The man yells as Rio nearly hits him again, flooring it and making the tires spin on the gravel before finding purchase and lurching the car forward just as the man leaps out of the way.

He slams a hand on the back window on Nicky's side as it passes, making him yelp in terror. Jen reaches over and cups the side of his head as if there were a physical threat, then turns to glare out the rear window as they leave him in the dust.

"You're fine," she tells Nicky, turning her attention back to him and smiling tightly.

"Who is that?" He asks, trying to crane around to see behind them, but Jen straightens him forward and fusses pointlessly with his seatbelt in distraction.

"Are you safe to go home?" Rio asks, eyes flicking up to the rearview mirror. Agatha turns to face Jen properly and takes in how genuinely disheveled she looks for the first time.

"Yeah," Jen breathes. She nods, catching Agatha's eye. She must look particularly uncharacteristically worried, because Jen clarifies. "Seriously. I have like a trillion alarms and cameras and my neighbors are total assholes about untoward visitors taking up all the good street parking. They'll show up to give me shit for violating the HOA before he has the chance to get through a window."

Agatha chuckles at the image and then steels her features into something more surly when Jen catches her amusement and smirks herself.

"Okay, but like… you can stay alone?" Rio clarifies. Agatha wonders who exactly she plans to volunteer for the job in the off chance that Jen says 'no', but luckily doesn't have the chance to find out.

"I'm fine. Really. You can take a left up here."

Agatha had only been to Jen's house twice before. Once, while picking up a package for her that had been sent to her doorstep instead of the shop during a busy week while Jen was out of town. The second had been while she was still in the throes of postpartum with Nicky, and he hadn't stopped crying until he'd literally lost his little voice. By day two of no sleep, Agatha probably shouldn't have been behind the wheel heading across town, but it had been worth it in the end to sleep on Jen's thousand-dollar couch in her white-noise-only townhouse with a no-lawnmowers-on-weekdays neighborhood mandate.

"You'll call if you need anything?" Agatha asks, if only to be polite.

"Sure," Jen replies, equally dishonest. She directs Rio down a few more blocks until they reach the curb, where two trash bins sit alone. Jen groans at the sight of a not-very-official-looking citation slapped to the side of one.

She doesn't bother with goodbye or goodnight as she steps out of the car, and Agatha turns her attention to Nicky, who has fallen asleep against her side, opting not to get into the front seat now that they're stopped.

"She actually is going to be okay, right?" Rio asks softly, though Agatha thinks the gentle tone is more for Nicky's sake than any genuine guilt or worry.

Instead of answering, she shoots Rio a grin.

"Can't believe you tried to commit vehicular manslaughter with my car."

Rio whines. Agatha's grin turns even more wolfish at the sound.

"I'm coming over," she says. "If you want me to pretend to be freaked out and too scared to stay alone in the shop, I can bat my eyes and put on a real show. But honestly, you just owe me three fingers and that thing you do with your thumb on my ass for saving you and your friend from that creep."

Agatha had started to cover Nicky's ears halfway through Rio's demand, before it even turned explicit, but he still squirms fussily and tucks into her more when her bark of surprised laughter upsets his dreams.

As tempting as the offer is, she's still feeling a little raw about the day. Not just Jen's stalker, but Billy and Alice and the apprenticeship. Nicky's solid weight against her side is the only thing that feels truly unimpeachable amidst the storm of unpredictability her life has become since that morning in her bathroom with Rio.

"I'll do you one better," she offers.

"Better than your—" Rio starts, but Agatha cuts her off.

"Gimme your number," she says, fishing her phone out of her pocket while trying not to jostle Nicky too much.

Rio's eyes light up.

"Really?"

"Yes," Agatha chuckles, her heart fluttering with what she irritably realizes is flattery.

"But I'll need yours, too. I'm not letting you scam me out of my number just to never call," Rio says, pointing to the mirror so her reflection jabs accusingly at Agatha as she narrows her eyes. "You could have just gotten my number from the client file at the shop, anyway."

"Are you accusing me of illegally soliciting confidential client data for personal gain? Sexual personal gain, even?" Agatha crows.

"Here," Rio responds, tossing her phone into Agatha's lap over her shoulder without answering her obvious bait to be teased. The car slows to a stop, and she realizes they're parked on the dark side of the street outside of Vidal's.

Agatha, true to her word for once, taps her number into the open contact page and reaches over to hand it to Rio.

"Thank you," Rio says, playfully gushing like an old movie star and inspiring Agatha to sock her in the bicep in retort. She holds out a hand expectantly for Agatha's phone and taps in her own number. Rio turns around fully in the driver's seat and kisses Agatha unexpectedly. It's a little clumsy, but it's sweet, and forces another not entirely unwelcome fluttering sensation through all of her veins at once.

"Goodnight," Agatha says unconvincingly.

Rio grins wolfishly, this time.

"Yeah. I'll call you."

She slips out of the car with a wink, and by the time Agatha has climbed out of the back seat without dislodging Nicky too much and situated herself in the still-warm driver's seat, Rio is gone, and the darkness of the flower shop offers no last glimpse of her.
